{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01XjdfTFCaDxV7v3jtZ6kQgr","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# PointClickCare Technologies Inc. — Product Research\n     2→\n     3→Researched: 2026-02-16\n     4→Developer website: https://pointclickcare.com/\n     5→\n     6→## Overview\n     7→\n     8→PointClickCare Technologies Inc. is a major cloud-based healthcare technology company specializing in long-term and post-acute care (LTPAC). Founded in 1995 (some sources say 2000) by Mike and Dave Wessinger, the company is headquartered in Mississauga, Ontario, Canada. With approximately 2,300 employees and $750M in annual revenue (as of 2025), PointClickCare is the dominant EHR vendor in the long-term care space — rated #1 Long-Term Care Software Provider by KLAS Research for six consecutive years through 2025. The company serves over 27,000 long-term and post-acute care providers, 3,100+ hospitals and health systems, 2,000+ ambulatory clinics, every major U.S. health plan, and 70+ state and government agencies. Their platform manages data for over 1.4 million active patients across 22,000+ skilled nursing facilities and 4,000+ assisted living facilities.\n     9→\n    10→PointClickCare has grown significantly through acquisitions: Collective Medical (2020, care coordination for acute/ambulatory/post-acute), Audacious Inquiry (2022, $650M, connected care network for data exchange), Patient Pattern (2023, value-based care platform for Medicare Advantage SNPs, PACE, ACO REACH), and American HealthTech (2024, long-term care EHR from CPSI). The company has also partnered with Apploi for staffing/scheduling capabilities. PointClickCare is privately held and has not gone public.\n    11→\n    12→The company's primary market is post-acute and long-term care: skilled nursing facilities (SNFs), assisted living, independent living, memory care, and continuing care retirement communities (CCRCs). They have expanded into adjacent markets including hospitals/health systems (for care transitions), ACOs, health plans, life sciences (de-identified data), pharmacies, and practice groups (physicians who serve LTPAC populations).\n    13→\n    14→## Product: PointClickCare (Core EHR Platform)\n    15→\n    16→CHPL ID: 10246\n    17→\n    18→### What It Is\n    19→\n    20→PointClickCare's core platform is a cloud-based, ONC-certified EHR designed primarily for skilled nursing facilities and senior living communities. It is not a single monolithic application but rather an integrated platform with numerous modules and add-ons. The certified product encompasses the core EHR functionality — clinical documentation, order management, medication administration, care planning, transitions of care, patient portal capabilities, and FHIR API access. The platform is certified across 27 criteria including clinical data (a)(1)–(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14), transitions of care (b)(1)–(b)(3), patient portal (e)(1), CQM (c)(1), and FHIR APIs (g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10).\n    21→\n    22→The certified module is a component of a much larger product ecosystem. The broader PointClickCare platform also includes revenue cycle management, analytics, staffing/scheduling (Apploi), CRM, care coordination network capabilities (from Collective Medical/Audacious Inquiry acquisitions), and numerous specialty modules.\n    23→\n    24→### Users & Market\n    25→\n    26→Day-to-day users include:\n    27→- **Nurses** (the primary charting users — documenting vitals, administering medications via eMAR, care planning, wound care tracking, shift-based documentation)\n    28→- **Medical Directors and Physicians** (order entry, encounter documentation, ePrescribing)\n    29→- **Clinical staff** (CNAs documenting ADLs, therapy staff)\n    30→- **MDS Coordinators** (completing and submitting MDS 3.0 assessments to iQIES)\n    31→- **Billing/Financial staff** (claims processing, accounts receivable, census management)\n    32→- **Administrators/Practice Managers** (dashboards, quality metrics, staffing)\n    33→- **Pharmacists** (via pharmacy integration for order management)\n    34→\n    35→The SED intended user description from CHPL metadata confirms: \"Clinicians, Nurses, Medical Directors, Other Healthcare Staff Roles.\"\n    36→\n    37→Notable scale: 27,000+ LTPAC providers, making it by far the largest EHR in the long-term care segment. Aside from Epic in the broader EHR market, PointClickCare has among the largest market penetration of any EHR vendor due to its dominance in LTC/SNF.\n    38→\n    39→### Modules & Functionality\n quality measures (CQMs), operational performance metrics.\n   134→\n   135→**The de-identified data catalog** (offered to life sciences companies) confirms the breadth of stored data: outcomes, biomarkers, diagnoses, co-morbidities, treatments, therapy, encounters, claims, MDS assessments, drug regimens, facility-level order data, HCP and pharmacy relationships. They claim \"more clinical datapoints per patient than CMS, Registry, Ambulatory or Acute EMR datasets\" with 10+ years of longitudinal data.\n   136→\n   137→**Lab and imaging**: The platform has \"Integrated Lab and Imaging\" listed as a value package for SNFs, meaning lab and imaging orders and potentially results flow through the system — but specifics on how results data is stored vs. referenced externally were not detailed in vendor materials.\n   138→\n   139→---\n   140→\n   141→## Product: EHR for Practice Groups\n   142→\n   143→CHPL ID: 11729\n   144→\n   145→### What It Is\n   146→\n   147→EHR for Practice Groups is PointClickCare's dedicated EHR offering for physician practice groups — typically practitioners who provide medical services to residents in skilled nursing and senior living facilities. This is a newer certified product (version 3, certified December 2025) that provides a practitioner-facing interface optimized for the workflows of physicians, nurse practitioners, and physician assistants who serve LTPAC populations. It is certified across 28 criteria — nearly identical to the core PointClickCare EHR, covering clinical data (a)(1)–(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14), transitions of care (b)(1)–(b)(3), patient portal (e)(1), CQM (c)(1), and FHIR APIs (g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10).\n   148→\n   149→This product appears to be closely related to the \"Practitioner Engagement\" module, which provides mobile and web access for practitioners to interact with PointClickCare's core EHR data. It is not a standalone EHR for general ambulatory practice — it is specifically designed for practice groups that serve the LTPAC population and need to interact with PointClickCare's core platform.\n   150→\n   151→### Users & Market\n   152→\n   153→The primary users are:\n   154→- **Physicians** serving SNF and senior living residents\n   155→- **Nurse Practitioners and Physician Assistants** providing care in LTPAC settings\n   156→- **Practice group administrators** managing documentation, billing, and value-based care arrangements\n   157→\n   158→PointClickCare reports serving 2,000+ ambulatory clinics, though it's unclear how many specifically use the \"EHR for Practice Groups\" product vs. the broader platform. The product targets practice groups engaged in value-based care arrangements (Medicare Advantage SNPs, PACE, ACO REACH) where documentation quality and care coordination directly affect reimbursement.\n   159→\n   160→### Modules & Functionality\n   161→\n   162→Based on the product page, solution sheet, and certifications page:\n   163→\n   164→**Clinical Documentation:**\n   165→- Automated workflows and auto-populated patient data for fast encounter documentation\n   166→- Single login access to the patient record (shared with the core PointClickCare EHR)\n   167→- Access to chart summaries including vitals, notes, lab results, medication history, progress notes\n   168→- Encounter note submission directly into the EHR\n   169→\n   170→**Order Management:**\n   171→- Electronic signing of telephone and verbal orders\n   172→- Bulk order review\n   173→- Discharge order management\n   174→- ePrescribing with EPCS compliance\n   175→- Drug interaction alerts and order templates with auto-populated information\n   176→\n   177→**Care Coordination:**\n   178→- Single patient record shared across the care continuum\n   179→- Communication and collaboration tools across care teams\n   180→- Support for care transitions\n   181→\n   182→**Value-Based Care Support:**\n   183→- Data and reporting for value-based care arrangements\n   184→- Metrics tracking: readmission reduction, ED utilization, chronic disease management, patient satisfaction\n   185→- Revenue acceleration through improved documentation\n   186→\n   187→**Security:**\n   188→- Multi-factor authentication across application access, clinical workflows, and SSO\n   189→\n   190→### Data & Content\n   191→\n   192→EHR for Practice Groups shares the same underlying patient record as the core PointClickCare platform. Practitioners access and contribute to the same clinical data — encounter notes, orders, prescriptions, vital signs, lab results, medication history, progress notes, chart summaries, and discharge information. The product adds the practitioner's documentation layer (encounter notes, orders, prescriptions) on top of the facility-based nursing and clinical documentation.\n   193→\n   194→Given the shared data model, the data types stored are essentially the same as the core PointClickCare EHR described above, with particular emphasis on practitioner-generated content: encounter notes, orders (medications, labs, imaging), prescriptions (including controlled substances), and value-based care metrics.\n   195→\n   196→The nearly identical certification criteria between this product and the core PointClickCare EHR (10246) — and the fact that they share the same EHI export documentation URL — strongly suggests these products share a common data platform.\n   197→\n   198→---\n   199→\n   200→## Research Notes\n   201→\n   202→**Gaps and Uncertainties:**\n   203→- The exact boundary between what's in the \"certified\" EHR vs. add-on modules is unclear. The CHPL certification covers the core EHR, but the broader platform includes many modules (CRM, staffing, analytics) that may or may not be part of the certified product's data scope for EHI export purposes.\n   204→- Lab and imaging result storage specifics were not clearly documented — it's listed as an \"Integrated Lab and Imaging\" value package, but whether results are stored natively or referenced from external systems is not detailed.\n   205→- The relationship between \"EHR for Practice Groups\" and \"Practitioner Engagement\" is somewhat ambiguous — they appear to be closely related or overlapping products.\n   206→- PointClickCare's late-2025 launch of a \"next-generation\" senior living EHR with AI-powered analytics may represent a platform evolution that affects what data is stored.\n   207→- The American HealthTech acquisition (2024) brought another LTC EHR into the fold — it's unclear whether that product's data has been migrated into or integrated with the PointClickCare platform.\n   208→\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. Founded in 1995 (some sources say 2000) by Mike and Dave Wessinger, the company is headquartered in Mississauga, Ontario, Canada. With approximately 2,300 employees and $750M in annual revenue (as of 2025), PointClickCare is the dominant EHR vendor in the long-term care space — rated #1 Long-Term Care Software Provider by KLAS Research for six consecutive years through 2025. The company serves over 27,000 long-term and post-acute care providers, 3,100+ hospitals and health systems, 2,000+ ambulatory clinics, every major U.S. health plan, and 70+ state and government agencies. Their platform manages data for over 1.4 million active patients across 22,000+ skilled nursing facilities and 4,000+ assisted living facilities.\n\nPointClickCare has grown significantly through acquisitions: Collective Medical (2020, care coordination for acute/ambulatory/post-acute), Audacious Inquiry (2022, $650M, connected care network for data exchange), Patient Pattern (2023, value-based care platform for Medicare Advantage SNPs, PACE, ACO REACH), and American HealthTech (2024, long-term care EHR from CPSI). The company has also partnered with Apploi for staffing/scheduling capabilities. PointClickCare is privately held and has not gone public.\n\nThe company's primary market is post-acute and long-term care: skilled nursing facilities (SNFs), assisted living, independent living, memory care, and continuing care retirement communities (CCRCs). They have expanded into adjacent markets including hospitals/health systems (for care transitions), ACOs, health plans, life sciences (de-identified data), pharmacies, and practice groups (physicians who serve LTPAC populations).\n\n## Product: PointClickCare (Core EHR Platform)\n\nCHPL ID: 10246\n\n### What It Is\n\nPointClickCare's core platform is a cloud-based, ONC-certified EHR designed primarily for skilled nursing facilities and senior living communities. It is not a single monolithic application but rather an integrated platform with numerous modules and add-ons. The certified product encompasses the core EHR functionality — clinical documentation, order management, medication administration, care planning, transitions of care, patient portal capabilities, and FHIR API access. The platform is certified across 27 criteria including clinical data (a)(1)–(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14), transitions of care (b)(1)–(b)(3), patient portal (e)(1), CQM (c)(1), and FHIR APIs (g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10).\n\nThe certified module is a component of a much larger product ecosystem. The broader PointClickCare platform also includes revenue cycle management, analytics, staffing/scheduling (Apploi), CRM, care coordination network capabilities (from Collective Medical/Audacious Inquiry acquisitions), and numerous specialty modules.\n\n### Users & Market\n\nDay-to-day users include:\n- **Nurses** (the primary charting users — documenting vitals, administering medications via eMAR, care planning, wound care tracking, shift-based documentation)\n- **Medical Directors and Physicians** (order entry, encounter documentation, ePrescribing)\n- **Clinical staff** (CNAs documenting ADLs, therapy staff)\n- **MDS Coordinators** (completing and submitting MDS 3.0 assessments to iQIES)\n- **Billing/Financial staff** (claims processing, accounts receivable, census management)\n- **Administrators/Practice Managers** (dashboards, quality metrics, staffing)\n- **Pharmacists** (via pharmacy integration for order management)\n\nThe SED intended user description from CHPL metadata confirms: \"Clinicians, Nurses, Medical Directors, Other Healthcare Staff Roles.\"\n\nNotable scale: 27,000+ LTPAC providers, making it by far the largest EHR in the long-term care segment. Aside from Epic in the broader EHR market, PointClickCare has among the largest market penetration of any EHR vendor due to its dominance in LTC/SNF.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nBased on vendor materials, product pages, and third-party reviews, the PointClickCare platform includes the following modules and capabilities:\n\n**Core EHR / Clinical Documentation:**\n- Point-of-care (POC) charting for nurses — vital signs, weights, allergies, immunizations, treatments, condition changes\n- Care plans with evidence-based protocols\n- Progress notes and encounter documentation\n- Physician order management (CPOE for medications, labs, imaging)\n- Diagnosis management\n- Assessment tools including MDS 3.0 with automated scheduling and iQIES submission\n- Nursing Advantage module — comprehensive assessments and proactive clinical guidance\n- Skin and wound care tracking module\n- Infection prevention and control module\n- Incident/event reporting\n\n**Medication Management (QuickMAR / eMAR):**\n- Electronic medication administration record\n- Bar-coded medication administration (BCMA) with scanning\n- Pharmacy order import and real-time connectivity with partner pharmacies\n- Allergy checking and drug interaction alerts\n- Omitted dose alerts\n- ePrescribing including EPCS (Electronic Prescriptions for Controlled Substances) — certified for 21 CFR §1311.300\n\n**Revenue Cycle Management (RCM):**\n- Claims processing for Medicare, Medicaid, and private insurers\n- PDPM (Patient-Driven Payment Model) support including \"PDPM Coach\" add-on\n- Accounts receivable tracking\n- Financial dashboards and reporting\n- Census management and budgeting\n- Trust fund management\n- General Ledger/Accounts Payable module\n- Billing optimization and collections workflows\n- RCM Services (outsourced claims processing offering)\n\n**Care Coordination & Transitions:**\n- Secure messaging (Secure Conversations module)\n- Hospital admission/readmission alerts (from Collective Medical acquisition)\n- Electronic discharge summary exchange with hospitals\n- Insurance coverage validation\n- Resident status tracking and event calendar\n- PAC Management tools for hospitals/health systems\n- Connected Care Center — care coordination network\n\n**Analytics & Reporting:**\n- Performance Insights / Advanced Insights modules — clinical, financial, operational dashboards\n- MDS Analytics\n- CMS 5-star rating tracking\n- Quality and compliance metrics\n- Regulatory reporting (OASIS/MDS quality metrics)\n- Market Insights — peer performance benchmarking against national averages\n- Clinical alerts (pressure ulcers, fall risks)\n\n**Senior Living-Specific:**\n- Companion mobile app for staff (ADL documentation in under 30 seconds)\n- Pharmacy Connect for medication order management\n- Senior Sign (digital move-in paperwork)\n- CRM for marketing/occupancy management\n- Nutrition Management / Mealtime Solutions\n- Document Manager\n- Billing with resident-friendly statements\n\n**Staffing & Operations:**\n- Apploi Hire/Onboard (recruitment)\n- Apploi Schedule (shift management)\n- Automated Care Messaging\n\n**Integrations & Interoperability:**\n- HL7 interfaces, APIs, FHIR-based connectors\n- 400+ marketplace integrations\n- Lab and imaging ordering integration\n- Pharmacy integration\n- Device integration (blood pressure monitors, etc.)\n- Virtual Health (telehealth) module\n\n**Patient/Family Engagement:**\n- Patient portal (View, Download, Transmit per (e)(1) certification)\n- Clinician and family portals\n- Automated care messaging to families\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on vendor documentation, product pages, and the de-identified data catalog, PointClickCare stores and manages the following types of data:\n\n**Clinical Data:** Diagnoses, co-morbidities, allergies, vital signs (blood pressure, temperature, weight, etc.), immunizations, care plans, progress notes, encounter notes, nursing documentation, physician orders, treatment records, condition change documentation, wound care tracking records, infection control data, incident reports, clinical alerts, assessments (including MDS 3.0), therapy data, biomarkers, disease-specific assessments.\n\n**Medication Data:** Medication orders, medication administration records (eMAR), drug regimens, prescription data (including controlled substances via EPCS), pharmacy orders, allergy alerts, drug interaction alerts, medication history.\n\n**Financial/Billing Data:** Claims (Medicare, Medicaid, private insurance), accounts receivable, census data, trust fund records, PDPM scoring data, billing statements, collections data, general ledger/accounts payable, budgets.\n\n**Administrative/Operational Data:** Staff scheduling, staffing records, recruitment/onboarding data, resident status, event calendars, insurance coverage information, digital move-in paperwork (Senior Sign), CRM/marketing data, facility-level data.\n\n**Care Coordination Data:** Discharge summaries, transitions of care documents, hospital admission/readmission alerts, care manager notes, referral data, insurance validation data.\n\n**Analytics/Reporting Data:** Quality metrics, CMS 5-star ratings, benchmarking data, OASIS/MDS quality measures, clinical quality measures (CQMs), operational performance metrics.\n\n**The de-identified data catalog** (offered to life sciences companies) confirms the breadth of stored data: outcomes, biomarkers, diagnoses, co-morbidities, treatments, therapy, encounters, claims, MDS assessments, drug regimens, facility-level order data, HCP and pharmacy relationships. They claim \"more clinical datapoints per patient than CMS, Registry, Ambulatory or Acute EMR datasets\" with 10+ years of longitudinal data.\n\n**Lab and imaging**: The platform has \"Integrated Lab and Imaging\" listed as a value package for SNFs, meaning lab and imaging orders and potentially results flow through the system — but specifics on how results data is stored vs. referenced externally were not detailed in vendor materials.\n\n---\n\n## Product: EHR for Practice Groups\n\nCHPL ID: 11729\n\n### What It Is\n\nEHR for Practice Groups is PointClickCare's dedicated EHR offering for physician practice groups — typically practitioners who provide medical services to residents in skilled nursing and senior living facilities. This is a newer certified product (version 3, certified December 2025) that provides a practitioner-facing interface optimized for the workflows of physicians, nurse practitioners, and physician assistants who serve LTPAC populations. It is certified across 28 criteria — nearly identical to the core PointClickCare EHR, covering clinical data (a)(1)–(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14), transitions of care (b)(1)–(b)(3), patient portal (e)(1), CQM (c)(1), and FHIR APIs (g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10).\n\nThis product appears to be closely related to the \"Practitioner Engagement\" module, which provides mobile and web access for practitioners to interact with PointClickCare's core EHR data. It is not a standalone EHR for general ambulatory practice — it is specifically designed for practice groups that serve the LTPAC population and need to interact with PointClickCare's core platform.\n\n### Users & Market\n\nThe primary users are:\n- **Physicians** serving SNF and senior living residents\n- **Nurse Practitioners and Physician Assistants** providing care in LTPAC settings\n- **Practice group administrators** managing documentation, billing, and value-based care arrangements\n\nPointClickCare reports serving 2,000+ ambulatory clinics, though it's unclear how many specifically use the \"EHR for Practice Groups\" product vs. the broader platform. The product targets practice groups engaged in value-based care arrangements (Medicare Advantage SNPs, PACE, ACO REACH) where documentation quality and care coordination directly affect reimbursement.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nBased on the product page, solution sheet, and certifications page:\n\n**Clinical Documentation:**\n- Automated workflows and auto-populated patient data for fast encounter documentation\n- Single login access to the patient record (shared with the core PointClickCare EHR)\n- Access to chart summaries including vitals, notes, lab results, medication history, progress notes\n- Encounter note submission directly into the EHR\n\n**Order Management:**\n- Electronic signing of telephone and verbal orders\n- Bulk order review\n- Discharge order management\n- ePrescribing with EPCS compliance\n- Drug interaction alerts and order templates with auto-populated information\n\n**Care Coordination:**\n- Single patient record shared across the care continuum\n- Communication and collaboration tools across care teams\n- Support for care transitions\n\n**Value-Based Care Support:**\n- Data and reporting for value-based care arrangements\n- Metrics tracking: readmission reduction, ED utilization, chronic disease management, patient satisfaction\n- Revenue acceleration through improved documentation\n\n**Security:**\n- Multi-factor authentication across application access, clinical workflows, and SSO\n\n### Data & Content\n\nEHR for Practice Groups shares the same underlying patient record as the core PointClickCare platform. Practitioners access and contribute to the same clinical data — encounter notes, orders, prescriptions, vital signs, lab results, medication history, progress notes, chart summaries, and discharge information. The product adds the practitioner's documentation layer (encounter notes, orders, prescriptions) on top of the facility-based nursing and clinical documentation.\n\nGiven the shared data model, the data types stored are essentially the same as the core PointClickCare EHR described above, with particular emphasis on practitioner-generated content: encounter notes, orders (medications, labs, imaging), prescriptions (including controlled substances), and value-based care metrics.\n\nThe nearly identical certification criteria between this product and the core PointClickCare EHR (10246) — and the fact that they share the same EHI export documentation URL — strongly suggests these products share a common data platform.\n\n---\n\n## Research Notes\n\n**Gaps and Uncertainties:**\n- The exact boundary between what's in the \"certified\" EHR vs. add-on modules is unclear. The CHPL certification covers the core EHR, but the broader platform includes many modules (CRM, staffing, analytics) that may or may not be part of the certified product's data scope for EHI export purposes.\n- Lab and imaging result storage specifics were not clearly documented — it's listed as an \"Integrated Lab and Imaging\" value package, but whether results are stored natively or referenced from external systems is not detailed.\n- The relationship between \"EHR for Practice Groups\" and \"Practitioner Engagement\" is somewhat ambiguous — they appear to be closely related or overlapping products.\n- PointClickCare's late-2025 launch of a \"next-generation\" senior living EHR with AI-powered analytics may represent a platform evolution that affects what data is stored.\n- The American HealthTech acquisition (2024) brought another LTC EHR into the fold — it's unclear whether that product's data has been migrated into or integrated with the PointClickCare platform.\n","numLines":208,"startLine":1,"totalLines":208}}}

--></p>\n   181→<div class=\"markdown-toc\">\n   182→<ul id=\"markdown-toc\">\n   183→  <li><a href=\"#background\" id=\"markdown-toc-background\">Background</a></li>\n   184→  <li><a href=\"#format\" id=\"markdown-toc-format\">Format</a></li>\n   185→  <li><a href=\"#resource\" id=\"markdown-toc-resource\">Resource</a></li>\n   186→</ul>\n   187→\n   188→</div>\n   189→<h3 id=\"background\">Background</h3>\n   190→\n   191→<p>PointClickCare supports the export of electronic health information (EHI).</p>\n   192→\n   193→<p>As of Version 4, PointClickCare’s Health IT modules, and only the modules listed below, are ONC 2015 Edition compliant. The ONC HIT Certification Program is a voluntary certification program established by the Office of the National Coordinator for Health IT to provide for the certification of health IT. The program supports initiatives such as Promoting Interoperability Programs (previously Medicare and Medicaid EHR Incentive Programs) used by eligible providers and hospitals for funding and the 21st Century Cures Act. The use of certified health IT has expanded to other government and non-government programs. Reference to the certification and a list of PointClickCare’s current certified modules can be <a href=\"https://pointclickcare.com/company/certifications/\">found here</a>.</p>\n   194→\n   195→<h3 id=\"format\">Format</h3>\n   196→<p>The export artifacts are encrypted compressed files. A single artifact is generated per patient for single export, or an artifact can contain multiple patients for population export. Please consult your PointClickCare documentation on obtaining the encryption keys.</p>\n   197→\n   198→<p>Please note, certain data elements in the patient file may include references to other records. Using the ID listed in the reference, further details on the referenced records can be found in the Index files.</p>\n   199→\n   200→<p>For example, under the Care Team data element, there could be references to Practitioner, PractitionerRole, Organization or Related Person as seen below:</p>\n   201→<pre><code style=\"color:#777\">{\"resourceType\":\"CareTeam\",\"id\":\"a080fbfe-a58a-4bc0-9504-6c1ce50304c0-17107600\",\"identifier\":[{\"system\":\"http://www.pointclickcare.com/careTeam/identifier\",\"value\":\"a080fbfe-a58a-4bc0-9504-6c1ce50304c0-17107600\"}],\"status\":\"active\",\"subject\":{\"reference\":\"Patient/a080fbfe-a58a-4bc0-9504-6c1ce50304c0-17107600\"},\"participant\":[{\"role\":[{\"coding\":[{\"system\":\"http://nucc.org/provider-taxonomy\",\"code\":\"207RG0300X\"}],\"text\":\"Allopathic &amp; Osteopathic Physicians : Internal Medicine : Geriatric Medicine\"}],\"member\":{\"reference\":\"Practitioner/<b style=\"color:black\">a080fbfe-a58a-4bc0-9504-6c1ce50304c0-26606-5</b>\"}},{\"role\":[{\"coding\":[{\"system\":\"http://nucc.org/provider-taxonomy\",\"code\":\"207RG0300X\"}],\"text\":\"Allopathic &amp; Osteopathic Physicians : Internal Medicine : Geriatric Medicine\"}],\"member\":{\"reference\":\"PractitionerRole/<b style=\"color:black\">a080fbfe-a58a-4bc0-9504-6c1ce50304c0-26606-5</b>\"}},{\"role\":[{\"coding\":[{\"system\":\"http://www.pointclickcare.com/careTeam/role\",\"code\":\"Sister-in-law\"}],\"text\":\"Sister-in-law\"}],\"member\":{\"reference\":\"RelatedPerson/<b style=\"color:black\">a080fbfe-a58a-4bc0-9504-6c1ce50304c0-1543640-17107600</b>\"}}],\"managingOrganization\":[{\"reference\":\"Organization/<b style=\"color:black\">a080fbfe-a58a-4bc0-9504-6c1ce50304c0</b>\"}]}\n   202→</code></pre>\n   203→\n   204→<p>A corresponding data element with the id reference can then be found in the index files:</p>\n   205→<pre><code style=\"color:#777\">{\"resourceType\":\"Practitioner\",\"id\":\"<b style=\"color:black\">a080fbfe-a58a-4bc0-9504-6c1ce50304c0-26606-5</b>\",\"identifier\":[{\"system\":\"http://www.pointclickcare.com/practitioner/identifier\",\"value\":\"a080fbfe-a58a-4bc0-9504-6c1ce50304c0-26606-5\"},{\"system\":\"http://hl7.org/fhir/sid/us-npi\",\"value\":\"1477519569\"}],\"active\":false,\"name\":[{\"use\":\"official\",\"family\":\"Good\",\"given\":[\"Jordan\"]}],\"telecom\":[{\"system\":\"phone\",\"value\":\"(145)354-5902\",\"use\":\"work\"}],\"gender\":\"male\"}\n   206→</code></pre>\n   207→\n   208→<p>Additionally, if there are Consents and Advanced Directive documents attached to a patient chart, they will be available through the 'Files' folder. Assessments will be available through two separate folders containing MDS and non-MDS Assessments.</p>\n   209→\n   210→<p>Radiology images and laboratory results attached to a patient chart are available through the 'radiology-attachments' and 'lab-attachments' folder.</p>\n   211→\n   212→<p>EHI is given as both standard FHIR R4 and non-FHIR custom resources. Custom resources conform to the basic requirements for any FHIR Base Resource with id and meta elements. \n   213→Data is output using <a href=\"https://build.fhir.org/nd-json.html\"> Newline Delimited JSON</a> formatted files or Comma-Separated Values (CSV).</p>\n   214→\n   215→<h3 id=\"resource\">Resource</h3>\n   216→<p><a href=\"toc.html\"> View list of all the exported artifacts</a></p>\n   217→\n   218→\n   219→\n   220→\n   221→</div>\n   222→        </div>  <!-- /inner-wrapper -->\n   223→      </div>  <!-- /row -->\n   224→    </div>  <!-- /container -->\n   225→  </div>  <!-- /segment-content -->\n   226→\n   227→  <script type=\"text/javascript\" src=\"assets/js/jquery.js\"> </script>     <!-- note keep space here, otherwise it will be transformed to empty tag -> fails -->\n   228→  <script type=\"text/javascript\" src=\"assets/js/jquery-ui.min.js\"> </script>\n   229→\n   230→  <script type=\"text/javascript\" src=\"assets/js/window-hash.js\"> </script>\n   231→  <a name=\"bottom\"> </a>\n   232→  <div id=\"segment-footer\" igtool=\"footer\" class=\"segment\">  <!-- segment-footer -->\n   233→    <div class=\"container\">  <!-- container -->\n   234→\n   235→      <div class=\"inner-wrapper\">\n   236→        <p>\n   237→          IG &#169; 2023+ <a style=\"color:var(--footer-hyperlink-text-color)\" href=\"https://www.pointclickcare.com\">PCC</a>. The ONC HIT Certification Program is a voluntary certification program established by the Office of the National Coordinator for Health IT to provide for the certification of health IT. The program supports initiatives such as Promoting Interoperability Programs (previously Medicare and Medicaid EHR Incentive Programs) used by eligible providers and hospitals for funding and the 21st Century Cures Act. The use of certified health IT has expanded to other government and non-government programs. Reference to the certification and a list of PointClickCare’s current certified modules can be <a href=\"https://pointclickcare.com/company/certifications/\">found here</a>.</p>\n\n<h3 id=\"format\">Format</h3>\n<p>The export artifacts are encrypted compressed files. A single artifact is generated per patient for single export, or an artifact can contain multiple patients for population export. Please consult your PointClickCare documentation on obtaining the encryption keys.</p>\n\n<p>Please note, certain data elements in the patient file may include references to other records. Using the ID listed in the reference, further details on the referenced records can be found in the Index files.</p>\n\n<p>For example, under the Care Team data element, there could be references to Practitioner, PractitionerRole, Organization or Related Person as seen below:</p>\n<pre><code style=\"color:#777\">{\"resourceType\":\"CareTeam\",\"id\":\"a080fbfe-a58a-4bc0-9504-6c1ce50304c0-17107600\",\"identifier\":[{\"system\":\"http://www.pointclickcare.com/careTeam/identifier\",\"value\":\"a080fbfe-a58a-4bc0-9504-6c1ce50304c0-17107600\"}],\"status\":\"active\",\"subject\":{\"reference\":\"Patient/a080fbfe-a58a-4bc0-9504-6c1ce50304c0-17107600\"},\"participant\":[{\"role\":[{\"coding\":[{\"system\":\"http://nucc.org/provider-taxonomy\",\"code\":\"207RG0300X\"}],\"text\":\"Allopathic &amp; Osteopathic Physicians : Internal Medicine : Geriatric Medicine\"}],\"member\":{\"reference\":\"Practitioner/<b style=\"color:black\">a080fbfe-a58a-4bc0-9504-6c1ce50304c0-26606-5</b>\"}},{\"role\":[{\"coding\":[{\"system\":\"http://nucc.org/provider-taxonomy\",\"code\":\"207RG0300X\"}],\"text\":\"Allopathic &amp; Osteopathic Physicians : Internal Medicine : Geriatric Medicine\"}],\"member\":{\"reference\":\"PractitionerRole/<b style=\"color:black\">a080fbfe-a58a-4bc0-9504-6c1ce50304c0-26606-5</b>\"}},{\"role\":[{\"coding\":[{\"system\":\"http://www.pointclickcare.com/careTeam/role\",\"code\":\"Sister-in-law\"}],\"text\":\"Sister-in-law\"}],\"member\":{\"reference\":\"RelatedPerson/<b style=\"color:black\">a080fbfe-a58a-4bc0-9504-6c1ce50304c0-1543640-17107600</b>\"}}],\"managingOrganization\":[{\"reference\":\"Organization/<b style=\"color:black\">a080fbfe-a58a-4bc0-9504-6c1ce50304c0</b>\"}]}\n</code></pre>\n\n<p>A corresponding data element with the id reference can then be found in the index files:</p>\n<pre><code style=\"color:#777\">{\"resourceType\":\"Practitioner\",\"id\":\"<b style=\"color:black\">a080fbfe-a58a-4bc0-9504-6c1ce50304c0-26606-5</b>\",\"identifier\":[{\"system\":\"http://www.pointclickcare.com/practitioner/identifier\",\"value\":\"a080fbfe-a58a-4bc0-9504-6c1ce50304c0-26606-5\"},{\"system\":\"http://hl7.org/fhir/sid/us-npi\",\"value\":\"1477519569\"}],\"active\":false,\"name\":[{\"use\":\"official\",\"family\":\"Good\",\"given\":[\"Jordan\"]}],\"telecom\":[{\"system\":\"phone\",\"value\":\"(145)354-5902\",\"use\":\"work\"}],\"gender\":\"male\"}\n</code></pre>\n\n<p>Additionally, if there are Consents and Advanced Directive documents attached to a patient chart, they will be available through the 'Files' folder. Assessments will be available through two separate folders containing MDS and non-MDS Assessments.</p>\n\n<p>Radiology images and laboratory results attached to a patient chart are available through the 'radiology-attachments' and 'lab-attachments' folder.</p>\n\n<p>EHI is given as both standard FHIR R4 and non-FHIR custom resources. Custom resources conform to the basic requirements for any FHIR Base Resource with id and meta elements. \nData is output using <a href=\"https://build.fhir.org/nd-json.html\"> Newline Delimited JSON</a> formatted files or Comma-Separated Values (CSV).</p>\n\n<h3 id=\"resource\">Resource</h3>\n<p><a href=\"toc.html\"> View list of all the exported artifacts</a></p>\n\n\n\n\n</div>\n        </div>  <!-- /inner-wrapper -->\n      </div>  <!-- /row -->\n    </div>  <!-- /container -->\n  </div>  <!-- /segment-content -->\n\n  <script type=\"text/javascript\" src=\"assets/js/jquery.js\"> </script>     <!-- note keep space here, otherwise it will be transformed to empty tag -> fails -->\n  <script type=\"text/javascript\" src=\"assets/js/jquery-ui.min.js\"> </script>\n\n  <script type=\"text/javascript\" src=\"assets/js/window-hash.js\"> </script>\n  <a name=\"bottom\"> </a>\n  <div id=\"segment-footer\" igtool=\"footer\" class=\"segment\">  <!-- segment-footer -->\n    <div class=\"container\">  <!-- container -->\n\n      <div class=\"inner-wrapper\">\n        <p>\n          IG &#169; 2023+ <a style=\"color:var(--footer-hyperlink-text-color)\" href=\"https://www.pointclickcare.com\">PCC</a>.          <td style=\"column-width:30%\">\n   364→               <a href=\"StructureDefinition-provenance.html\"\n   365→                  title=\"StructureDefinition/provenance\">Provenance</a>\n   366→            </td>\n   367→            <td>\n   368→<p>Provenance of a resource is a record that describes entities and processes involved in producing and delivering or otherwise influencing that resource. Provenance provides a critical foundation for assessing authenticity, enabling trust, and allowing reproducibility. Provenance assertions are a form of contextual metadata and can themselves become important records with their own provenance. Provenance statement indicates clinical significance in terms of confidence in authenticity, reliability, and trustworthiness, integrity, and stage in lifecycle (e.g. Document Completion - has the artifact been legally authenticated), all of which may impact security, privacy, and trust policies.</p>\n   369→</td>\n   370→         </tr>\n   371→         <tr>\n   372→            <td Provenance statement indicates clinical significance in terms of confidence in authenticity, reliability, and trustworthiness, integrity, and stage in lifecycle (e.g. Document Completion - has the string;\n    valueSet?: string;\n  };\n}\n\ninterface ProfileInfo {\n  id: string;\n  name: string;\n  type: string;\n  kind: string;\n  description: string;\n  url: string;\n  baseDefinition?: }[];\n}\n\ninterface Output {\n  extractionDate: string;\n  igPackage: string;\n  igVersion: string;\n  fhirVersion: string;\n  profiles: ProfileInfo[];\n  valueSets: ValueSetInfo[];\n  codeSystems: strength: el.binding.strength,\n                    valueSet: el.binding.valueSet,\n                  },\n                }\n              : {}),\n          }));\n\n        // Detect custom resources:  resource.type !== \"Extension\";\n\n        const extensionUrls = diff\n          .filter((el: any) =>\n            el.type?.some((t: any) => t.code === \"Extension\" && t.profile)\n          )\n customResources.length,\n      standardResourceCount: standardResources.length,\n      valueSetCount: valueSets.length,\n      codeSystemCount: codeSystems.length,\n    },\n  };\n\n  await strength: el.binding.strength,\n                    valueSet: el.binding.valueSet,\n                  },\n                }\n              : {}),\n          }));\n\n        // Detect custom resources: kind=resource but type is not a standard FHIR R4 type\n        const standardFhirTypes = new Set([\n          \"AllergyIntolerance\", \"CarePlan\", \"CareTeam\", \"Condition\", \"Coverage\",\n      concepts.push({ code: c.code, display: c.display || \"\" });\n          }\n        }\n        valueSets.push({\n          id: resource.id,\n          name: resource.name,\n          url: Extracted to `downloads/package/package/` — contains 135 JSON files including 67 StructureDefinitions, 2 ValueSets, 2 CodeSystems, the ImplementationGuide resource, and validation artifacts.\n\n**Step 6: Downloaded all 76 HTML pages**\n\nAll StructureDefinition, ValueSet, CodeSystem, MDS, NonMDS, index, TOC, and artifacts pages downloaded to `downloads/site/`.\n\n**Step 7: Enrichment extraction**\n\nRan two Bun TypeScript scripts to extract structured data from the package JSON and HTML pages:\n- `structure-definitions.json`: 67 profiles, 2 value sets, 2 code systems — zero parse failures\n- `csv-schemas.json`: 12 CSV schemas with 225 total columns — zero parse failures\n\n## What Was Found\n\nPointClickCare has built a genuine FHIR R4 Implementation Guide specifically for their EHI Export. This is one of the most thorough and well-structured (b)(10) export documentation sets encountered. The IG goes well beyond repackaging a (g)(10) FHIR API — it defines custom FHIR-like resources for data categories that have no standard FHIR representation, and exports assessments in CSV format.\n\n### Export Format\n\nThe export produces **encrypted compressed files** containing:\n\n1. **FHIR R4 NDJSON files** — one per resource type per patient, for both standard and custom resources\n2. **CSV files** — for MDS (Minimum Data Set) and non-MDS clinical assessments\n3. **Attached files** — consent/advance directive documents in a `Files` folder, radiology images in a `radiology-attachments` folder, lab attachments in a `lab-attachments` folder\n\nThe directory structure per patient is:\n```\n[Patient ID]/\n  [Patient ID].json              # Patient FHIR resource\n  [Patient ID]-manifest.json     # Manifest of export contents\n  mds_assessments/\n    [Assessment ID]/\n      assessment.csv, sections.csv, sectionV.csv,\n      pdpmScores.csv, score.csv, sectionVRapProfile.csv, responses.csv\n  non_mds_assessments/\n    [Assessment ID]/\n      assessment.csv, sections.csv, score.csv,\n      responses.csv, sdoh_and_health_status_responses.csv\n  Files/                         # Consent and advance directive documents\n  radiology-attachments/         # Radiology images\n  lab-attachments/               # Lab result attachments\n```\n\nEncryption keys are obtained through PointClickCare documentation (not publicly specified in the IG).\n\n### Standard FHIR R4 Resource Profiles (26)\n\nThese are constrained profiles on standard FHIR resources:\n\n| Resource | Description |\n|----------|-------------|\n| AllergyIntolerance | With PCC extensions for RecordedBy, RevisionBy, ResolvedDate, RevisionDate |\n| CarePlan | Patient care plans |\n| CareTeam | Care team with cross-references to Practitioner, PractitionerRole, Organization, RelatedPerson |\n| Condition | Diagnoses and conditions |\n| Coverage | Insurance coverage with PCC extensions for PayerType, PayerDescription, MedicareAdvantagePlan |\n| DiagnosticReport | Lab and diagnostic results |\n| DocumentReference | Progress notes, advanced directives, consent documents, radiology reports, skin/wound docs, therapy files |\n| Encounter | Patient encounters (admissions/visits) |\n| FamilyMemberHistory | Family health history |\n| Goal | Care plan goals |\n| Immunization | With PCC extension for ImmunizationStatus |\n| Device (Implantable) | Implantable devices |\n| Location | PCC-specific facility/location info |\n| Medication | Drug details with RxNorm coding, form, strength, UOM |\n| MedicationDispense | Pharmacy dispensing records |\n| MedicationRequest | Orders with dose quantity, frequency, timing, indications, pharmacy details; extensive extension set (RouteOfAdministration, ScheduleStartDate, TimeCode, AdministeredBy, OrderCategory, OrderType, OrderedBy, Context, Custodian) |\n| Observation | Vitals, occupation, smoking status, pregnancy status/intent |\n| Organization | Facility/organization info |\n| Patient | Demographics with US Core extensions (race, ethnicity, birth sex, gender identity, sex, tribal affiliation, pronouns) plus PCC extensions (PreviousName, Occupation, CompanyName) |\n| Practitioner | Provider info |\n| PractitionerRole | Provider roles |\n| Procedure | Procedures performed |\n| Provenance | Record provenance/audit trail |\n| RelatedPerson | Patient contacts/relationships |\n| ServiceRequest | Lab, imaging, and other service requests with LOINC order codes |\n| Specimen | Lab specimens |\n\n### Custom (Non-FHIR) Resources (10)\n\nThese are PCC-specific resource types that conform to FHIR Base Resource structure but define entirely new schemas for data domains that don't map to standard FHIR:\n\n| Custom Resource | Description | Key Fields |\n|----------------|-------------|------------|\n| **Authorization** | Prior authorization records | payer, insurance, start/end dates, review dates, request status, authorization number, remaining days/visits |\n| **BillingStatement** | Patient billing statements | statement date, invoice transactions, balance due, previous balance, payments, current charges, total due |\n| **CareProfile** | Patient care profile questionnaires | category name, question text, value |\n| **Census** | Patient census/admission tracking | 27 fields including action, payer, status, admission/discharge dates, room/bed, physician, payor plan, RAP billing flags, LOA (leave of absence) data |\n| **Claim** | Insurance claims | 29 fields including status, revenue code, HIPPS code, service dates, charges, units, diagnosis codes, DRG, attending physician |\n| **InvoiceTransaction** | Individual billing line items | effective date, description, units, unit amount, amount |\n| **Order** | Non-pharmacy orders (lab, diet, imaging, immunization) | 36 fields including category, status, dates, diet details, lab details, communication method, LOINC codes |\n| **Payment** | Payment records | receipt amount, trust amount, payer, posting date, check info, payment type |\n| **medicalDevice** | Medical devices (non-implantable) | auxiliary device, device treatment (each with sub-elements) |\n| **singleDevice** | Individual device records | device name, created by, detail |\n\n### Custom Extensions (31)\n\nPCC defines 31 extensions covering medication administration details, ordering metadata, payer information, patient demographics, and clinical tracking fields. These are well-documented with proper FHIR extension definitions.\n\n### CSV Assessment Schemas\n\n**MDS Assessments (7 CSV files, 166 columns total):**\n- `assessment.csv` (90 columns) — Comprehensive MDS 3.0 assessment metadata including RUG scores, PDPM data, ADL indices, CAT triggers, CMI weights, HIPPS codes, submission batches\n- `sections.csv` (9 columns) — Assessment section metadata\n- `sectionV.csv` (9 columns) — Section V specific data (discharge planning)\n- `pdpmScores.csv` (35 columns) — Patient-Driven Payment Model scoring across all five PDPM components (PT, OT, SLP, nursing, NTA) plus non-case-mix\n- `score.csv` (2 columns) — RUG/PDPM scoring summaries\n- `sectionVRapProfile.csv` (6 columns) — RAP (Resident Assessment Protocol) profiles\n- `responses.csv` (15 columns) — Individual MDS question responses\n\n**Non-MDS Assessments (5 CSV files, 59 columns total):**\n- `assessment.csv` (25 columns) — Assessment metadata\n- `sections.csv` (9 columns) — Section data\n- `score.csv` (2 columns) — Scoring\n- `responses.csv` (9 columns) — Question-level responses\n- `sdoh_and_health_status_responses.csv` (14 columns) — SDOH and health status assessment responses\n\n### Value Sets and Code Systems\n\n- **Immunization Status** — PCC-defined: Historical, Active, Inactive, Discontinued, Refused, Not Applicable\n- **Order Status** — PCC-defined: Active, Cancelled, Completed, Discontinued, Not Applicable, Pending, Voided\n\n### Dependencies\n\nThe IG depends on:\n- HL7 FHIR R4 Core (4.0.1)\n- HL7 Terminology R4 (7.0.1)\n- HL7 FHIR UV Extensions R4 (5.2.0)\n- US Core 3.1.1\n- mCODE 1.0.0 (Minimal Common Oncology Data Elements — interesting dependency for an LTPAC product)\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nThis is an **unusually comprehensive** EHI export for a certified EHR. Based on the product research, PointClickCare stores data across clinical, medication, financial/billing, administrative, care coordination, and analytics domains. The export covers:\n\n**Clearly covered domains:**\n- **Demographics and contacts** — Patient resource with extensive extensions (race, ethnicity, gender identity, pronouns, occupation, previous names)\n- **Diagnoses/conditions** — Condition resource\n- **Medications** — Three resources: Medication (drug details), MedicationRequest (orders with detailed dosing/timing), MedicationDispense (pharmacy dispensing)\n- **Allergies** — AllergyIntolerance with PCC-specific tracking extensions\n- **Lab results** — DiagnosticReport, Observation, ServiceRequest, Specimen; plus lab attachments folder\n- **Vital signs** — Observation resource\n- **Immunizations** — Immunization resource with status extension\n- **Procedures** — Procedure resource\n- **Care plans and goals** — CarePlan, Goal resources\n- **Clinical notes** — DocumentReference covers progress notes, radiology reports, skin/wound docs, therapy files\n- **Encounters** — Encounter resource\n- **Care team** — CareTeam resource with cross-references\n- **Family history** — FamilyMemberHistory\n- **Insurance/coverage** — Coverage resource with payer type, Medicare Advantage plan extensions\n- **Prior authorizations** — Custom Authorization resource with remaining days/visits tracking\n- **Billing statements** — Custom BillingStatement with InvoiceTransaction line items\n- **Insurance claims** — Custom Claim resource with 29 fields (revenue codes, HIPPS, DRG, charges, diagnosis codes)\n- **Payments** — Custom Payment resource\n- **MDS 3.0 assessments** — Exported as CSV with 7 files per assessment, 166 total columns covering all MDS data including PDPM scores, RUG calculations, ADL indices, CATs — this is critical for LTPAC and is excellently documented\n- **Non-MDS clinical assessments** — All other assessments exported as CSV, including SDOH and health status data\n- **Census/admission data** — Custom Census resource with 27 fields covering admission, discharge, transfers, leave of absence, bed/room, physician, payer\n- **Care profiles** — Custom CareProfile resource for questionnaire-type data\n- **Orders (non-pharmacy)** — Custom Order resource with 36 fields for diet, lab, diagnostic, and immunization orders\n- **Medical devices** — Custom medicalDevice and singleDevice resources\n- **Documents and consents** — DocumentReference plus Files folder for consent and advance directive documents\n- **Radiology images** — Dedicated radiology-attachments folder\n- **Provenance** — Provenance resource for record tracking\n\n**Potentially missing or unclear domains:**\n- **Medication administration records (eMAR)** — The product has a sophisticated eMAR (QuickMAR), but the export has MedicationRequest and MedicationDispense but no explicit MedicationAdministration resource. Medication administration may be partly captured via the AdministeredBy extension on MedicationRequest, or it may not be exported at the individual administration level.\n- **Wound care tracking** — Mentioned as a DocumentReference type (skin/wound files) but may lack structured wound assessment data (measurements, staging).\n- **Incident/event reports** — Not mentioned in the export documentation; these could be in the non-MDS assessments.\n- **Trust fund management** — Not explicitly documented as a separate export; could be partially in Payment records.\n- **Therapy data** — Therapy files referenced in DocumentReference, but structured therapy minutes/outcomes (critical for PDPM PT/OT/SLP scoring) are not clearly documented as a separate export — though PDPM scores are in the MDS CSV.\n- **Secure messaging content** — The Secure Conversations module data doesn't appear to be exported.\n- **External records incorporated into chart** — Not explicitly mentioned.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export format is **FHIR R4 NDJSON plus CSV**, which is an excellent choice for a (b)(10) export:\n\n- **Standard FHIR R4** for data that maps well to FHIR resources (clinical data, demographics, medications, etc.)\n- **Custom FHIR-like resources** for data that doesn't fit standard FHIR (billing, claims, census, orders, devices) — these follow FHIR Base Resource structure with id and meta, making them parseable with FHIR tooling even though they're non-standard\n- **CSV** for assessment data (MDS and non-MDS) — this is appropriate because MDS data is inherently tabular and question-response oriented\n- **File attachments** for documents, radiology images, and lab results\n\nThe use of custom resources for billing/financial data is a strength — PCC could have omitted billing data (as many vendors do) or crammed it into ill-fitting standard FHIR resources. Instead, they created purpose-built schemas.\n\nCross-references between resources use FHIR references (e.g., CareTeam members reference Practitioner/PractitionerRole/Organization/RelatedPerson IDs), and index files provide lookup tables for referenced entities.\n\nThe FHIR version is R4 (4.0.1). US Core 3.1.1 is a dependency. The mCODE 1.0.0 dependency is notable but its impact on the export is not explicitly documented.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- The documentation is a proper FHIR Implementation Guide built with standard HL7 tooling — the same tooling used for official HL7 IGs. This means it comes with machine-readable StructureDefinitions, proper navigation, and a familiar format for FHIR developers.\n- The FHIR IG NPM package is published and downloadable — this is a machine-readable artifact that can be loaded into FHIR tooling for validation.\n- 67 StructureDefinitions with snapshot and differential views provide complete field-level documentation.\n- CSV schemas are documented with column names, data types, nullable flags, and descriptions.\n- The index page explains the export format, directory structure, and cross-referencing mechanism with worked examples.\n- Custom resources are clearly identified as \"PointClickCare specific\" in their descriptions.\n- Recently updated (2026-02-04 generation date, 2026-02-09 last modified).\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- Version 0.1.0 with \"ci-build\" status and `notForPublication: true` in the package metadata — this is technically a draft/development build, not a published release. The \"Draft as of 2026-02-04\" label confirms this.\n- No sample export files or example resources are provided — a developer implementing an import would need to work entirely from the StructureDefinitions.\n- No explicit user guide for how to request/perform an export — the documentation describes what comes out but not how to trigger it.\n- Encryption key distribution is mentioned but not documented.\n- The descriptions for some custom resources are minimal (e.g., CareProfile: \"Patient CareProfile\").\n- No changelog or versioning beyond 0.1.0.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n**Granularity:** Field-level documentation is comprehensive for most resources. Each element has a path, data type, cardinality (min/max), short description, and in many cases detailed definition. Binding information links to value sets where applicable. The MDS assessment CSV schemas have 90 columns for the main assessment file alone, with clear type and nullable specifications.\n\n**Coded fields:** Some coded fields are documented with proper value set bindings (immunization status, order status). Others use reference to external coding systems (RxNorm for medications, NUCC for provider taxonomy, LOINC for observations and orders, ICD-10 for diagnoses, HCPCS for claims).\n\n**Relationships:** Cross-references between resources are documented through the FHIR reference mechanism. The index page explains how to resolve references using index files. Parent-child relationships (e.g., BillingStatement → InvoiceTransaction, Census → payer details) are expressed through FHIR references.\n\n**Overall assessment:** This is one of the strongest (b)(10) export documentation efforts observed. PointClickCare has clearly invested real engineering effort in their EHI export — they haven't simply repackaged their (g)(10) FHIR API. The custom resource definitions for billing, claims, census, and orders demonstrate genuine engagement with the (b)(10) requirement to export *all* EHI, not just the USCDI/US Core clinical subset. The use of the FHIR IG toolchain means the documentation is both human-readable (via the HTML site) and machine-readable (via the NPM package), which is a significant advantage.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://ehi-export.pointclickcare.com/site/index.html\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (all content accessible via curl)\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (two-page site: Home + Artifacts, plus individual resource pages)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n\nNone. The documentation was immediately accessible, well-structured, and included a downloadable FHIR IG package. The URL had a Google Analytics tracking parameter (`_ga=...`) that had no effect on accessibility. Extracted to `downloads/package/package/` — contains 135 JSON files including 67 StructureDefinitions, 2 ValueSets, 2 CodeSystems, the ImplementationGuide resource, and validation artifacts.\n\n**Step 6: Downloaded all 76 HTML pages**\n\nAll StructureDefinition, ValueSet, CodeSystem, MDS, NonMDS, index, TOC, and artifacts pages downloaded to `downloads/site/`.\n\n**Step 7: Enrichment extraction**\n\nRan two Bun TypeScript scripts to extract structured data from the package JSON and HTML pages:\n- `structure-definitions.json`: 67 profiles, 2 value sets, 2 code systems — zero parse failures\n- `csv-schemas.json`: 12 CSV schemas with 225 total columns — zero parse failures\n\n## What Was Found\n\nPointClickCare has built a genuine FHIR R4 Implementation Guide specifically for their EHI Export. This is one of the most thorough and well-structured (b)(10) export documentation sets encountered. The IG goes well beyond repackaging a (g)(10) FHIR API — it defines custom FHIR-like resources for data categories that have no standard FHIR representation, and exports assessments in CSV format.\n\n### Export Format\n\nThe export produces **encrypted compressed files** containing:\n\n1. **FHIR R4 NDJSON files** — one per resource type per patient, for both standard and custom resources\n2. **CSV files** — for MDS (Minimum Data Set) and non-MDS clinical assessments\n3. **Attached files** — consent/advance directive documents in a `Files` folder, radiology images in a `radiology-attachments` folder, lab attachments in a `lab-attachments` folder\n\nThe directory structure per patient is:\n```\n[Patient ID]/\n  [Patient ID].json              # Patient FHIR resource\n  [Patient ID]-manifest.json     # Manifest of export contents\n  mds_assessments/\n    [Assessment ID]/\n      assessment.csv, sections.csv, sectionV.csv,\n      pdpmScores.csv, score.csv, sectionVRapProfile.csv, responses.csv\n  non_mds_assessments/\n    [Assessment ID]/\n      assessment.csv, sections.csv, score.csv,\n      responses.csv, sdoh_and_health_status_responses.csv\n  Files/                         # Consent and advance directive documents\n  radiology-attachments/         # Radiology images\n  lab-attachments/               # Lab result attachments\n```\n\nEncryption keys are obtained through PointClickCare documentation (not publicly specified in the IG).\n\n### Standard FHIR R4 Resource Profiles (26)\n\nThese are constrained profiles on standard FHIR resources:\n\n| Resource | Description |\n|----------|-------------|\n| AllergyIntolerance | With PCC extensions for RecordedBy, RevisionBy, ResolvedDate, RevisionDate |\n| CarePlan | Patient care plans |\n| CareTeam | Care team with cross-references to Practitioner, PractitionerRole, Organization, RelatedPerson |\n| Condition | Diagnoses and conditions |\n| Coverage | Insurance coverage with PCC extensions for PayerType, PayerDescription, MedicareAdvantagePlan |\n| DiagnosticReport | Lab and diagnostic results |\n| DocumentReference | Progress notes, advanced directives, consent documents, radiology reports, skin/wound docs, therapy files |\n| Encounter | Patient encounters (admissions/visits) |\n| FamilyMemberHistory | Family health history |\n| Goal | Care plan goals |\n| Immunization | With PCC extension for ImmunizationStatus |\n| Device (Implantable) | Implantable devices |\n| Location | PCC-specific facility/location info |\n| Medication | Drug details with RxNorm coding, form, strength, UOM |\n| MedicationDispense | Pharmacy dispensing records |\n| MedicationRequest | Orders with dose quantity, frequency, timing, indications, pharmacy details; extensive extension set (RouteOfAdministration, ScheduleStartDate, TimeCode, AdministeredBy, OrderCategory, OrderType, OrderedBy, Context, Custodian) |\n| Observation | Vitals, occupation, smoking status, pregnancy status/intent |\n| Organization | Facility/organization info |\n| Patient | Demographics with US Core extensions (race, ethnicity, birth sex, gender identity, sex, tribal affiliation, pronouns) plus PCC extensions (PreviousName, Occupation, CompanyName) |\n| Practitioner | Provider info |\n| PractitionerRole | Provider roles |\n| Procedure | Procedures performed |\n| Provenance | Record provenance/audit trail |\n| RelatedPerson | Patient contacts/relationships |\n| ServiceRequest | Lab, imaging, and other service requests with LOINC order codes |\n| Specimen | Lab specimens |\n\n### Custom (Non-FHIR) Resources (10)\n\nThese are PCC-specific resource types that conform to FHIR Base Resource structure but define entirely new schemas for data domains that don't map to standard FHIR:\n\n| Custom Resource | Description | Key Fields |\n|----------------|-------------|------------|\n| **Authorization** | Prior authorization records | payer, insurance, start/end dates, review dates, request status, authorization number, remaining days/visits |\n| **BillingStatement** | Patient billing statements | statement date, invoice transactions, balance due, previous balance, payments, current charges, total due |\n| **CareProfile** | Patient care profile questionnaires | category name, question text, value |\n| **Census** | Patient census/admission tracking | 27 fields including action, payer, status, admission/discharge dates, room/bed, physician, payor plan, RAP billing flags, LOA (leave of absence) data |\n| **Claim** | Insurance claims | 29 fields including status, revenue code, HIPPS code, service dates, charges, units, diagnosis codes, DRG, attending physician |\n| **InvoiceTransaction** | Individual billing line items | effective date, description, units, unit amount, amount |\n| **Order** | Non-pharmacy orders (lab, diet, imaging, immunization) | 36 fields including category, status, dates, diet details, lab details, communication method, LOINC codes |\n| **Payment** | Payment records | receipt amount, trust amount, payer, posting date, check info, payment type |\n| **medicalDevice** | Medical devices (non-implantable) | auxiliary device, device treatment (each with sub-elements) |\n| **singleDevice** | Individual device records | device name, created by, detail |\n\n### Custom Extensions (31)\n\nPCC defines 31 extensions covering medication administration details, ordering metadata, payer information, patient demographics, and clinical tracking fields. These are well-documented with proper FHIR extension definitions.\n\n### CSV Assessment Schemas\n\n**MDS Assessments (7 CSV files, 166 columns total):**\n- `assessment.csv` (90 columns) — Comprehensive MDS 3.0 assessment metadata including RUG scores, PDPM data, ADL indices, CAT triggers, CMI weights, HIPPS codes, submission batches\n- `sections.csv` (9 columns) — Assessment section metadata\n- `sectionV.csv` (9 columns) — Section V specific data (discharge planning)\n- `pdpmScores.csv` (35 columns) — Patient-Driven Payment Model scoring across all five PDPM components (PT, OT, SLP, nursing, NTA) plus non-case-mix\n- `score.csv` (2 columns) — RUG/PDPM scoring summaries\n- `sectionVRapProfile.csv` (6 columns) — RAP (Resident Assessment Protocol) profiles\n- `responses.csv` (15 columns) — Individual MDS question responses\n\n**Non-MDS Assessments (5 CSV files, 59 columns total):**\n- `assessment.csv` (25 columns) — Assessment metadata\n- `sections.csv` (9 columns) — Section data\n- `score.csv` (2 columns) — Scoring\n- `responses.csv` (9 columns) — Question-level responses\n- `sdoh_and_health_status_responses.csv` (14 columns) — SDOH and health status assessment responses\n\n### Value Sets and Code Systems\n\n- **Immunization Status** — PCC-defined: Historical, Active, Inactive, Discontinued, Refused, Not Applicable\n- **Order Status** — PCC-defined: Active, Cancelled, Completed, Discontinued, Not Applicable, Pending, Voided\n\n### Dependencies\n\nThe IG depends on:\n- HL7 FHIR R4 Core (4.0.1)\n- HL7 Terminology R4 (7.0.1)\n- HL7 FHIR UV Extensions R4 (5.2.0)\n- US Core 3.1.1\n- mCODE 1.0.0 (Minimal Common Oncology Data Elements — interesting dependency for an LTPAC product)\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nThis is an **unusually comprehensive** EHI export for a certified EHR.
